# Unfilter digest 06 — pregnancy, birth and the first weeks

Read on 2026-11-17. Share: 368 quotes from `voice/reddit-ur12.jsonl`, 353 from `voice/reddit-ur26.jsonl` and 443 from `voice/reddit-ur33.jsonl`: **1,164 quotes**. All quote fields were read, together with each ledger’s **What these posts add** section and the current findings’ structure and conclusions. The comparisons below refer to `synthesis/findings.md` as restored on 2026-11-16.

These are quotes selected from previously excluded material, **not a prevalence sample**. All counts here count quote records or cited ids, never people. Several excerpts can belong to one post or one author; linked updates, replies and crossposts are not independent corroboration. Medical interpretations, risk estimates, reported laws, institutional policies, advice, product effects and accusations remain attributed testimony. No network or git was used.

The main change is to the planning-led reader portrait. Loss, decisions about continuation, coercion, severe psychiatric distress and postpartum life without a living baby need explicit treatment. The existing restored conclusions about consent, recovery, trust and limited personalization are confirmed, but the older shorthand about mental load, experience and a cooperative family plan still needs rewriting. Concrete requests in this share do not establish willingness to buy the proposed Mamivio guide.

Full excerpts below are copied directly from the record’s `quote` field. Repeated support is cross-referenced so the same passage need not be copied under every applicable pattern. Further supporting ids identify additional records after each pattern; their placement is a reading aid, not an exclusive interpretation of the quote.

## 1. Pregnancy number does not tell you how many children are alive or which stages the parent has experienced.

**Fits:** §§2 and 9; §10 Against 5; §§16.5 and 17.3.

**Relation: Extends, and contradicts the unrevised shorthand.** Current wording: “Second-timers already know and are afraid because they know”

**What should change:** Separate pregnancy count, births, living children, losses, gestation reached, NICU history and current care of another child. A ninth pregnancy can still precede the first living baby; staying pregnant can consume the time otherwise used to prepare. Keep the restored qualification and rewrite the older shorthand.

**Verbatim evidence**

> I just need to rant and to not feel alone. Currently i'm 7w4d with my 4th pregnancy (no living children) and I am just going through it. I suffered with nausea during my two viable pregnancies (one was a chemical) so i'm no stranger to it, but this pregnancy is definitely hitting me way harder. Don't get me wrong, I am extremely grateful for all my symptoms and just for being pregnant because my losses hit me really hard. I even prayed to feel this again, but now that it's here I feel like a shell of a person. I don't want to eat because everything is gross, drinking water is hard, everything leaves a gross lingering aftertaste in my mouth, i'm constipated from my zofran, the smell of my fridge makes me want to throw it all away, and I can barely go into my basement without the usual basement smell making me sick. My husband has been a God send when it comes to help, but i'm usually the person who takes care of others so it's extremely hard for me to sit back and have him do it all. Again, I am SO thankful for this pregnancy and for it going well, but I am struggling to be taken care of and from all the symptoms. 
<!-- quote-end -->

I know there isn't much to do at this point but I am going to go crazy not knowing if my baby is okay for another month as my first pregnancy resulted in a mmc (I miscarried at 7w2d and didn't know for another 3 weeks). I just want to know that I made it past my first milestone but unfortunately i'm being made to wait. Pregnancy is hard and we as women are absolute superhero's for doing this.
<!-- quote-end -->

**Further supporting ids:** None beyond the excerpts and cross-references above.

## 2. Tests, scans, symptoms, devices, feeds and AI can move the fear to the next milestone instead of settling it.

**Fits:** §4 Apps / Google, AI and Reddit; §6 Waiting, reassurance, stories, search and AI; §§16.4 and 17.6.

**Relation: Confirms and extends.** Current wording: “More information can reassure or renew fear.”

**What should change:** Name the mechanisms: symptoms improving can look like loss, a reassuring scan can last days, home-test darkness can become a repeat test, dates can conflict with apps, and an unexplained wearable alert or chatbot probability can create a fresh alarm. Real prior losses and unresolved diagnoses are part of this, not a generic excess of tabs. Preserve requests for positive-only stories, requests for both outcomes, and accounts where research or less checking helps.

<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0289]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0289 -->

She said that the "mass in my fallopian tube" was just a weird fibroid sticking out of the outside of my uterus, and that they found a tiny gestational sac in the right spot. I still have to follow up closely, but they are now saying it's \*not\* ectopic. 
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0290]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0290 -->

I just hope nothing else goes wrong - my anxiety is definitely having a field day with this whole incident, and hope I'm not going through this only to find out that something else is wrong later on. <!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0318]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0318 -->

Both of my previous pregnancies (ended in miscarriages at 9&11 weeks) I did not experience such frequent cramping. It’s not debilitating I’m still able to function but just scared it’s a sign of something. I’m quite convinced I’m having aMMC and just not bleeding yet due to the progesterone :(. <!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0371]

## 3. Preparing for birth and the first weeks also means miscarriage, TFMR, stillbirth, neonatal death and grief without a living baby.

**Fits:** §2 Birth / The first two weeks; §4 preparation; §6 fears; new subsection for loss and bereavement.

**Relation: Extends and contradicts a universal living-newborn arc.** Current wording: “Make the first two weeks a full section: feeding, sleep, recovery supplies, the partner's shifts, visitors.”

**What should change:** Add preparation for diagnosis confirmation, miscarriage management, D&C, labor and recovery after TFMR or stillbirth, comfort care, returning to a prepared home without a baby, changing a twin registry after one dies, and grief while caring for another child. Do not turn these accounts into a neat path from loss to healing or assume the physical birth itself was the traumatic part.

**Verbatim evidence**

[pbu12-0043]

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0043 -->
> I'm currently 4 weeks+ 5 days pregnant. Had my blood test this past Monday, all good. I will have an ultrasound this upcoming Wednesday. Last night I suddenly saw some light brown discharge, not much at all but still it shocked me. Today it has continued and it is a tiny bit pink but still it's not much at all. Is this normal? I've read that as long as it's not bright red fresh blood then you're fine...but still I'm worried (of course.) This is my first pregnancy and I don't know what to expect.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu12-0044]

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0044 -->
> Update 2: it ended up becoming a miscarriage

[pbu12-0056]

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0056 -->
> Hello everyone, I am having a D&C for my missed miscarriage this Friday. I have never had any kinda surgery and they’re giving me general anesthesia so I don’t know what to expect at all. Unfortunately the surgery is scheduled on my birthday, and after everything that has happened with the loss this feels like the ultimate slap in the face by the universe. What should I expect with the surgery, and how do I ever overcome the bitterness I feel towards everything that’s happened??
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu12-0133]

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0133 -->

I just want to lay on the floor and not move for a month, tell the whole world to fuck off. But I can't, he's there "Daddy, will you play with me?", "Daddy, let's play restaurant", "Daddy, be big dragon", "Daddy, catch me". How do you find time to grieve? How do you not let the little one know that everything \*will\* be okay but mommy and daddy are just really sad for a while? Fuck.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu12-0137]

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0137 -->

[pbu26-0111]

<!-- quote-start:pbu26-0111 -->
> The stories move neatly from loss to healing, from diagnosis to recovery, from fear to joy. What often gets omitted is the extent to which these things can coexist. The fear does not always disappear when the danger passes. The grief does not vanish when something wonderful happens afterward. More often, people simply learn how to carry both at once.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu26-0112]

<!-- quote-start:pbu26-0112 -->

[pbu33-0009]

<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0022]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0022 -->

I can barely get out of bed.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0061]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0061 -->
> At 46, after trying one more time, I find myself disappointed again. I became pregnant a few weeks ago, and I thought that prayer was finally answered. Yesterday, I found out that is not true. I have something I had never even heard of: a blighted ovum. A blighted ovum is when a pregnancy fails to develop and you end up with just an empty gestational sac. You get all the emotions and pregnancy symptoms, but no baby. It is like a beautifully wrapped present that has nothing in it. I was so happy; I thought this was my miracle. It turns out it is not.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0062]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0062 -->
> Yesterday, at 33 weeks, she went into labor. It was so quick and unexpected, I had just been speaking to her about her birth plan the day before and she hadn't yet made some of the decisions. We thought we had more time.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0063]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0063 -->
> I hadn't seen anything like that before. I know and accept in my heart that any and all kinds of things can happen during a birth. But... the way she looked at me when the NICU team was working on him... it scared me. I had no idea if I was doing the right thing, trying to soothe her, trying to distract her, trying to be a buoy in her most tumultuous sea.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0190]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0190 -->
> The third doctor told me "I'm going to be honest with you. I don't see a heartbeat and the baby is smaller than it should be." I pretty much tuned out after that. I just nodded and nodded and let them get out what they needed to say. I do appreciate her direct approach. I'm not one who likes it when people sugarcoat things. She told me if I wasn't interested in keeping the pregnancy, this is the time where they would give me options to terminate. Otherwise, I could wait another week to be sure and if there's still no heartbeat, I would need to decide if I wanted my body to naturally releases the fetus or if I preferred a medical or surgical abortion. From what I gathered when I was tuning in and out is that this is The Silent Miscarriage. The body doesn't recognize that the fetus stopped growing and/or died so it continues as if there is a pregnancy when there's not. You basically gone on happily making plans not knowing that what you're carrying is no longer living and in my case hasn't been for about three weeks. Based on the certainty of the exact dates, the size of the fetus and no heartbeat she offered the option for the abortion then and there but I opted to wait another week.
<!-- quote-end -->

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0191 -->

## 4. Wanting children, having planned conception and wanting to continue this pregnancy are different things.

**Fits:** §1 reader portrait; §2 stage arc; §6 shame and regret; §9 segments; §10 guide hypothesis.

**Relation: Extends and contradicts an assumed settled intention to continue.** Current wording: “She is pregnant, often for the first time”

**What should change:** Include undecided parents and parents choosing continuation, abortion or adoption; changed feelings after a planned conception; timing, freedom, religion, previous abortion and survival concerns. Some want the baby intensely and also want an abortion to live. Others remain distressed despite supportive families. Do not assume fetal movement, a scan, a shower or wanted conception will create attachment.

**Verbatim evidence**

> Is it too late to decide to have an abortion? I’m 15 weeks today living in a state where it’s “not legal”. I just feel like this is possibly my true last chance to decide before it’s genuinely too late. Im not ready to put my life on hold to become a mother. I’m not exactly looking for reassurance that everything will be okay, but more of advice on if anyone has decided to end a pregnancy this late in without having it be medically necessary. I don’t even feel guilty making this post which makes me feel like maybe It’s the right decision. No judgement pls this is marketed as a safe space
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0329]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0329 -->
> Unfortunately I felt extremely pressured by everyone around me including the father who I am no longer with, the fact that I already have a 6 year old daughter, and am low income. I resigned myself to accepting that this was the choice I made and went to sleep. The next day I woke up realizing what a terrible mistake I’d made and did some research. I found an agency called APR (abortion pill reversal) who got me in contact with a doctor nearby, who then explained the process and risks to me, and proceeded to prescribe me progesterone as an attempt to reverse the mifepristone. I took the first dose at 7pm that night, almost a full 24 hours later. The whole process was very quick. I then got in contact with multiple pregnancy centres in my area, who were all incredibly kind and understanding, and are still in touch following along this process with me. I have access to ongoing emotional counselling regardless of the outcome, which I am very grateful for.  
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0330]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0330 -->
> For the first 3 days I was directed to take 400mg of progesterone twice a day, and after three days 400mg once a day until 12 weeks if all is well. I am aware the risks of taking mifepristone without misoprostol, however pregnancy is never without risk, and I want to give my baby whom I already deeply love the chance to live. I wanted to post because there is really not much information about this process online, and I think anyone who is pro choice deserves to know there is a chance they could go on to have a healthy baby. There is no known risk of birth defects from taking progesterone, and about a 60-68% chance the reversal is successful. 
<!-- quote-end -->

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0358 -->

## 5. Some partner problems are violence, sexual or reproductive coercion, threats and dependence that a task list cannot repair.

**Fits:** §5 partners; §6 privacy and fear; §10 partner recommendation; §17.3 and historical gap in §17.7.

**Relation: Contradicts the older mental-load emphasis; extends the restored range.** Current wording: “From her side, the gap is mental load more than absence”

**What should change:** Give coercion and unsafe support their own treatment: pressure to abort or conceive, nonconsensual condom removal, unwanted touching, aggression, controlling money, custody or housing threats, and fear of leaving. Report accounts by partners and pregnant parents who admit aggression too. A cooperative family plan cannot be the default response to these circumstances.

## 6. Partners can share the work and grief, become overwhelmed themselves, or admit to withdrawing and causing harm.

**Fits:** §5 partners; §17.1 For 6; §17.6 contrary evidence.

**Relation: Confirms and extends.** Current wording: “The gap includes practical support during pain, illness and recovery and partners’ own fear and exhaustion.”

**What should change:** Retain capable, active partners and parents' own descriptions of responsibility. Add paternal grief while caring for a toddler, being left outside emergency surgery without information, solo newborn care during maternal readmission, and admitted coldness or verbal aggression. Short formats can help a willing learner but do not resolve resentment, exhaustion or safety constraints.

**Verbatim evidence**

> Last night, after a full day of trying to get everything done, the baby wouldn’t stop scream-crying, and I snapped. I yelled, “Shut the fuck up,” at him. When my wife understandably got angry, I called her a fucking bitch.
> 
> I’ve never spoken to either of them like that before. It felt unreal afterward. I was exhausted and overwhelmed, but neither of those things excuses what I said. My son was just being a baby, and my wife didn’t deserve that.  
> I’ve apologized profusely, but I still feel absolutely horrible. The guilt has gotten so intense that I’ve caught myself feeling like I don’t deserve to be alive. That part scares me too.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu12-0217]

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0217 -->
> His perspective seems to be that if I need something, I should ask. My perspective is that I don't want to have to ask for every single thing. I want to feel noticed, considered and cared for without constantly directing him.
> 
> One thing that keeps coming back to me is the saying that a woman never forgets how she was treated during pregnancy. I don't know how true that is, but I find myself thinking about it a lot. Pregnancy and postpartum were times when I felt I needed the most care, support and thoughtfulness from my husband, and some of my deepest hurt comes from how alone I felt emotionally during that period.
<!-- quote-end -->

<!-- quote-end -->

> He acknowledges that he isn’t meeting my needs. He tells me that he feels like a “husk of himself” and that he can barely support his own needs, let alone mine. He says he knows it isn’t fair to me, but despite these conversations, I haven’t really seen any action toward changing things.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu12-0305]

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0305 -->

[pbu12-0336]

So I waited a few minutes and got up pretending like I wasn’t in pain or about to pass out to tend to our newborn. In no way am I blaming him, I actually appreciate how much he’s been doing for us, and I already know he would never complain to my face or make me feel like a burden but I can’t help feeling like absolute shit being so helpless.

When they cut me open, her, my placenta and a liter of blood were in my abdomen. She was ultimately born safe and doing well with APGAR scores of 7 & 9 and just needed a day in the NICU as precaution and observation. They were able to repair my uterus and I did not need a hysterectomy.

[pbu33-0068]

[pbu33-0069]

**Further supporting ids:** [pbu12-0202] [pbu12-0277] [pbu33-0001]

## 7. Fear of being judged, investigated, discriminated against or separated from the baby can prevent truthful disclosure and care-seeking.

**Fits:** §5 Being believed; §6 Who to ask; §9 segments; §10 personalization.

**Relation: Extends.** Current wording: “The earlier contacts-and-checklists account understated loss of trust and the reluctance to ask again.”

**What should change:** Add explicit barriers to disclosure: a past safeguarding intervention, autism being interpreted as suspicious behavior, a trans man not recognized as the patient, sexual-assault history, drug-testing fears, prescribed-medication stigma, and wanting to speak privately without a partner. These are attributed experiences and fears, not verified legal consequences or clinical assessments.

**Verbatim evidence**

[pbu12-0015]

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0015 -->
> It’s already hard to present as a guy and be pregnant and I am not even far along yet. Almost every time I call the drs office they insist I have the wrong number or that they need to speak to the patient. It is definitely harder to be pregnant while not being female presenting and I am scared of experiencing discrimination or something. I found out that even in this day and age black women experience discrimination while pregnant, so imagine a pregnant man? Drs already overlook medical concerns I have, and it’s bothersome, but now in 8 months or less I will go through what will probably be the most physically painful thing I have experienced yet, and I am not the only one being affected now.
<!-- quote-end -->

It’s worse because I can’t talk to my mom about it, and besides my partner she is kind of the only person I would want to talk to and be comforted by during this whole thing.

**Further supporting ids:** None beyond the excerpts and cross-references above.

## 8. Care navigation needs explanations, confirmation, usable thresholds and completed follow-up, rather than only a number to call.

**Fits:** §5 Being believed; §6 Who to ask; §§16.2, 17.2 and 17.4 care navigation.

**Relation: Confirms and extends.** Current wording: “a 'who does what, who to call' page per country”

**What should change:** Keep the contact page but distinguish access from what happens after contact: conflicting scans, uncertain viability, missing orders and referrals, pressure to choose immediately, instructions that do not fit daily symptoms, scheduling contrary to a consultant's request, and no explanation of changing urgency. Include prompt, explained, compassionate care and second assessments that change an initial conclusion.

**Verbatim evidence**

[pbu12-0023]

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0023 -->
> During my 10 week blood tests I had a reactive titre of 1:1 and a positive RPR. The RPR (non-treponemal screening) test is typically first line of screening for syphilis, which can cause pretty significant harm to the baby (and you) if it's passed to them at birth. Of course I freaked out, I had never had syphilis or behaviors that put me at higher risk of contracting syphilis.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu12-0024]

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0024 -->
> My TP-PA ultimately came back negative for syphilis. In conversation with my doctor he mentioned that RPR is much faster and cheaper but comes with a high false positive rate since it is non-specific. Patients are typically tested twice in pregnancy and sometimes after delivery,  so for my future tests he made a note to skip the RPR and go straight for TP-PA to avoid any hassle or worry.
<!-- quote-end -->

Nurse disappears, maybe 10 minutes later a doctor comes in… deafening silence AGAIN and a stack of papers. She starts off with “Your baby is not good” and it immediately sends me into full blown panic attack with a heart rate of almost 200 and she’s telling me to calm down. She said my baby was no longer moving at all, had poor muscle response, and was pretty much unresponsive in utero. She told me I needed to sign papers asap for Emergency C-section.
<!-- quote-end -->

My OB immediately asked me to start on 200mg progesterone suppositories and come back for weekly monitoring along with doing pelvic rest and modified bed rest. > Today, I had an appointment with a doctor that I usually don’t see at my clinic. I was nervous. They knew I had had a previous missed miscarriage in the second trimester. Anywho, so they go to do the vaginal ultrasound and she says “ok here’s baby…” and then looks around to find the heart beat. I hold it together for a few seconds… and few seconds more pass, and she’s pressing on my bladder with her hand. I start to tear up, I immediately say, “There’s no heart beat is there?” and she is still pressing and trying to find something. My husband grabs my hand. We’ve been through this before. We’re bracing ourselves for that phrase where they say we’re sorry we can’t find the heart beat. I am going through all the emotions and thinking about how I’m going to have to call everyone again. I’m near crumbling. The doctor notices I’m upset and she says “don’t freak out” and she’s trying to press more on my belly. I’m like, wow ok… thanks for the words of wisdom doc. She says, I think “I see a flicker, but I’m not sure if that’s your heart beat” so she grabs my wrist to count the beats. I’m in shambles. 
<!-- quote-end -->

**Further supporting ids:** [pbu12-0233] [pbu12-0350] [pbu26-0152] [pbu26-0153] [pbu26-0364] [pbu33-0370]

## 9. Birth trauma can concern disbelieved pain, anaesthesia, separation and loss of consent even when the baby survives.

**Fits:** §2 Birth; §4 Birth preferences and Consent; §6 birth fear; §9 previous birth.

**Relation: Confirms and extends.** Current wording: “A flexible plan helps some people, but the earlier flexibility conclusion cannot explain every outcome or place responsibility for traumatic care on the woman.”

**What should change:** Show the concrete contingencies people were unprepared for: feeling cutting before general anaesthesia, waking no longer pregnant without seeing delivery, a partner excluded from theatre, no golden hour, unasked-for amniotomy, delayed recognition of bleeding and debriefing gaps. Keep clarifications that teams did discuss Caesarean birth, and positive induction or hospital rescue in the same account.

**Verbatim evidence**

I keep feeling guilty that I somehow let my baby down by - I don’t know, this makes no sense - by “letting” my body fail her. She was born at 39 weeks but she was not ready. Even reading my guilt makes no sense as it is not my fault that weather happens and baby poops itself and I need induction and a cascade of other medication in result. And I was high as hell on this opioid and I didn’t know exactly what was happening in the labour room. I asked my husband how long the last phase of pushing took, once the head was already visible and going back and forth, and he said about 20 minutes of midwife instructing me to control pushes to not tear myself, and I’ve read that is should have been few minutes at most. However there were 2 gynos and 2 midwives in the room and they wouldn’t risk the baby health, right? I just can’t shake the feeling of guilt off. <!-- quote-start:pbu33-0131 -->
> How is this even treated as a “simple condition”?  
> Even when I have my midwives appointments I feel how of little importance my concerns and discomfort comes across to them. As if all I should be shouting is “thank god I’m pregnant and my baby is well”. To simply ignore what my body is going through, to forget I’ve spent these months as a zombie and that my vag will probably be stitched up in about two months and I should be thankful- as long as baby is healthy.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0132]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0132 -->
> I don’t want to come across as a moaning, weak, pathetic woman who can’t even be pregnant but it all feels unreal to me. How little I was told about the real experience prior to getting pregnant. I knew it would probably be uncomfortable but I never knew how mentally it would affect me - and how painful and frustrating it would be.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0257]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0257 -->
> I am currently 33w pregnant after having a TFMR at 15w due to limb body wall complex last year. For context, I have moderate scoliosis as well as scar tissue buildup in my upper lumbar/lower thoracic area from a spinal injury a number of years ago with ongoing issues as a result. In both pregnancies, I have asked about epidurals and their success and both times (by different doctors/care teams) that an epidural likely won’t be successful, and if it is, could likely be patchy or not take fully. My pain tolerance is relatively low, and while I would love to be able to go without an epidural at all from the get go, I’m doubtful I can handle it but of course have no choice if that situation arises. 
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0258]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0258 -->
> In my brain, I’m really worried about going past my due date as baby keeps getting bigger, and with a low likelihood of the epidural, sounds absolutely terrible to go through unmedicated. Is an elective induction worth asking about? At 39w baby is a bit smaller than 41w, and having to birth a slightly smaller baby potentially all natural is honestly ideal should an epidural not be successful. 
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0259]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0259 -->
> My whole pregnancy has been low risk after 20w, no GD, but have measured 2 weeks ahead on and off. We also live 3+ hours away from any and all friends/family (we’re in SoCal, they’re in Canada), so c-section recovery would be really challenging when my husband goes back to work and we don’t have any support. Thoughts?
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0360]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0360 -->
> I was overdosed on Pitocin, I was overdosed on pain medication, and my child came out with withdrawal symptoms. My experience in the C-section was horrible, and despite getting an epidural during the induction and a spinal block during the C-section, I could still feel parts of it. When I would cry and scream in pain during the C-section and report this to the doctor, he told me I was just feeling pressure and pulling and that “most 16 year olds don’t know what they’re supposed to be feeling”. I passed out fully from the pain.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0361]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0361 -->
> There were a ton of other things that happened during labor that I feel contributed to the overall stress I was having, and it resulted in an insane amount of postpartum depression, and ultimately, diagnosed postpartum psychosis where I was convinced someone was going to try and take my baby away.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0362]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0362 -->
> Women who wanted VBac but ended up choosing csection because of your risk factors, how did you cope? I’m scared of struggling to bond with my baby while healing from a C-section, I’m scared of breast-feeding during a C-section recovery, I’m scared of limited mobility because of C-section recovery, I’m scared of feeling everything during the C-section. I’m just scared, this isn’t what I wanted.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0363]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0363 -->
> I am definitely pro listening to my doctor. I’ve been on the side of healthy and live birth, I just never pictured it would come down to a C-section, as silly as it seems. I appreciate him laying out the risks very straightforward to me.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0369]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0369 -->
> I also nearly died after childbirth due to retained placenta which led to Sepsis and I also had a postpartum Hemmorhage 24 hours after birth, it took me 8 weeks to recover enough to just carry my son from one side of the room to another, took me another 5 months to feel normal again.
<!-- quote-end -->

## 10. Perinatal distress can include suicidal thoughts, self-harm, intrusive harm thoughts, hallucinations and depression that available support has not relieved.

**Fits:** §2 recovery; §6 Fear, shame and identity; §7 advice limits; §9 personalization.

**Relation: Extends beyond ordinary fear or overwhelm.** Current wording: “The restored words include panic, sadness, lack of excitement, body distress, regret and fear of not bonding or surviving birth.”

**What should change:** Name the reported severity and the actual attempts: therapy, medication, EMDR, TMS, DBT, sleep and support can fail or be hard to obtain; other accounts describe improvement and peace during pregnancy. Preserve distinctions between intrusive thoughts, wanting not to exist, stated suicidal intent and diagnosed psychosis. These are personal accounts; neither a healthy baby nor an involved partner establishes that the parent is well.

**Verbatim evidence**

[pbu12-0061]

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0061 -->
> I just had my second baby 2 months ago and it was…traumatic to say the least. Had to be induced due to undiagnosed gestational diabetes and hypertension, during labor there was shoulder dystocia, then postpartum hemorrhage, then baby had to be in NICU. Baby is doing great now, and I’m fine now physically. But mentally it is ROUGH. I don’t want to hurt myself, but I just don’t want to exist. I haven’t shaken the baby but the thought comes in my mind and it freaks me the heck out. I want to divorce my husband for quite literally no reason. And I have such little feeling toward my oldest now. I feel HORRIBLE. They drive me crazy now and I want nothing to do with them. That’s not freaking normal and I know it isn’t, but I still feel that way towards them??
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu12-0062]

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0062 -->
> But therapy isn’t working. Meds aren’t working. EMDR isn’t working. Having “me time” or getting extra sleep or quite literally ANYTHING isn’t working. And the feelings are just getting worse and worse. Am I at a point where I need to voluntarily go to an inpatient facility for extra help?? I genuinely don’t know what to do, and I am spiraling and so scared. If anyone has any advice, please, please share.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu12-0077]

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0077 -->
> I remember the first few days home from giving birth and how I thought, “Is this my baby? When will his real parents show up?” How could I have ever thought that? Why was I crying and panicked all the time? Why didn’t I just enjoy him when he was small? I feel so guilty.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu12-0078]

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0078 -->
> Even though I largely feel okay right now, is it worth it to seek mental health support to process the past? I’m just devastated that I’ll never get that time back.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu12-0103]

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0103 -->
> It doesn’t just feel like “I’m tired because I’m pregnant.” I feel heavy, slowed down, emotionally flat, disconnected from myself & family, and like my brain isn’t giving me the motivation/reward feeling I used to have. Then I obsess over how different I feel and get scared that I’m permanently changed.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu12-0104]

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0104 -->
> And most importantly **did you eventually feel like yourself again, either later in pregnancy or postpartum?**
> 
> I would really appreciate hearing recovery stories because right now it’s hard not to feel like this is permanent 😭
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu12-0114]

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0114 -->
> Long story short- I had back to back pregnancy losses in 2021. 2022 was a horrible year for my mental health- I had sudden anxiety and intrusive suicidal thoughts (never wanting to act on them bit they were every second). So as someone who never had mental health issues - this was very scary for me.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu12-0118]

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0118 -->
> The PPD hit me hard around week 3. I couldn’t take care of my son and I just cried all the time. I couldn’t eat, I threw up from anxiety and lost 25 lbs. My boobs were oversupplying for my son. He wouldn’t latch. He cried all the time. Turns out he had an ear infection at his one week appointment. (He actually got several over the course of a few months and we ended up with tubes at 9 months old, praise the Lord that worked)
> 
> After talking with my doctor, he recommended I quit pumping for my mental health (my son wouldn’t latch because my let downs would choke him). Stopping pumping helped, but then later on I felt bad for quitting. Anyways, I’m thankful for sisters (they all have kids and no one experienced anything I did) and family to come over and help me with my son for a few weeks.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu12-0120]

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0120 -->
> I think the OB mentioned it could take a couple weeks to stabilize? In your experience did it take that long ? My husband returns to work next week. I asked another family member to fly in and help next week (also have a toddler) because I’m truly scared to be home alone with both kids at this point. 
> 
> When beginning to stabilize did you start to feel more like yourself? This isn’t how I imagined my Pp to go, I am missing out on my family time and ultimately my goal of BF. It’s all just so heavy and so much. I feel like I’m drowning.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu12-0123]

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0123 -->

I’ll be shaking, my heart rate will go up and I have to focus and breathe for it to calm down. Even after that, I still feel shaky and on edge. 
> 
> Has anything like this ever happened to anyone else? I will probably call my OB nurse line tomorrow but I’m really freaked out and would like to get some sleep tonight. Dealing with all of this + a newborn has been ruthless and I just want to feel better.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu12-0270]

I feel like some days I’m one step away from a grippy sock vacation. It’s not helping I’m just home alone all day with nothing but my craziness for company lol
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu12-0326]

>

[pbu26-0081]

> i remember coming home, exhausted and in pain from my c section. i slept in the rocker in my child’s room for the first 2 weeks because it was easier to get up (also had to pee every 20 mins because i was on lasix) but my body would NOT let me sleep. every noise, either from the house creaking, the wind blowing, or my cat running around, my eyes would pop open. i went 72 hours on no sleep. that’s when everything went nuts for me. for the next 6-8 weeks, i was hallucinating on and off. i would see ladybugs all over. i would hear my baby crying and even though i was right there, it was almost like i was running through a tunnel that would never end to get to her. i would attempt to go to the kitchen to freeze my milk, and get lost in my own house and panic because i didn’t know where to go. i just heard cries but i couldn’t move. i would hallucinate that my baby wasn’t in her bassinet and i couldn’t find her, but i heard her crying. the worst one was seeing a shadow figure standing somewhere in the room, telling me it was going to take my baby if i didn’t try harder. (YES HUSBAND WAS INVOLVED AND WAS UP EVERY NIGHT AND EVEN TRIED TO TAKE THE WHOLE NIGHT FOR ME TO SLEEP, but my mind would not let me).
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu26-0082]

<!-- quote-start:pbu26-0082 -->
> Eventually, through the help of my wonderful husband, my village, and sticking to my medication regime (and lots of therapy), i made it to the other side. but there were still “shifts” when the depression and anxiety hit harder, especially around 6-8 months pp.

[pbu26-0101]

I don’t think I ever want to try have another child, I’ve felt suicidal and part of me has hoped I miscarry.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu26-0118]

<!-- quote-start:pbu26-0118 -->
> With my second, I felt the PPD creeping in about two weeks pp and, while not as bad as with my first, decided to talk to my OB about treatment options at my 6 week appointment. Since I don’t struggle with depression outside of post partum, she recommended Zurzuvae coupled with talk therapy.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu26-0119]

<!-- quote-start:pbu26-0119 -->
> I started feeling the positive effects on day 2! I went from crying pretty much every day to only crying three times during the two weeks (and two of those times were related to my aunt who happened to to pass away the day after I started Zurzuvae). Sitting here right now, I can actually say I’m happy and enjoying my baby! (I didn’t enjoy my first born until he was like 5 months old!) I don’t dread the days and I don’t even get the evening scaries anymore. I’m excited for all the stages and phases to come, and I’m so grateful and took the leap with Zurzuvae. I’m interested to see if this feeling lasts - but as of now, I’m taking each day as it comes. The two weeks on the medicine were tough, but so far very worth it.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu26-0123]

<!-- quote-start:pbu26-0123 -->
> Ever since they told me that in hospital I’ve felt really sad about it and it’s starting to really affect my ability to cherish this newborn bubble for the last time. I feel a horrible sense of existential dread, can’t sleep, start thinking we are all just going to die one day and feel like life is pointless.   
> I get so incredibly sad that this is the last of everything, the last time I’ll have a baby this small, the last first bath, the last time I’ll do or experience everything with her. I lie awake long after she’s asleep and just want to cry, my husband goes straight to sleep and I just feel so empty, hopeless and alone.
<!-- quote-end -->

> I’m trying so hard to nourish my baby, but at the same time I’m terrified of gaining too much weight. I hate feeling hungry, but I also hate feeling full because then I feel guilty.

I have gotten a referral for a termination but I feel like people are going to resent and hate me for this decision. I have people saying it’s just hormones etc. but I’ve been checking in with my GP weekly and having psychology appointments weekly as well so I am actively seeking help but I genuinely have never felt so horrible and depressed in my entire life. I feel as though because I’ve been TTC I should be grateful and excited for this journey but I genuinely think I have made a big mistake and glorified motherhood in my own head.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0082]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0082 -->

[pbu33-0093]

> since becoming a parent 3 months ago, it is unbelievably devastating and harrowing to see or hear about any kind of threat or harm coming to kids, especially young babies, to the point it's starting to affect my ability to function and is causing nausea and panic attacks. I am thinking I need to step away from social media for awhile or at least see if I can put some filters on it, because I can no longer bear the amount of horrific news articles I see about young children experiencing medical struggles/injuries, being subjected to violence, being treated with abuse and/or neglect.
<!-- quote-end -->

## 11. Symptoms and treatment tradeoffs decide what a parent can do; medication avoidance is not a neutral baseline.

**Fits:** §2 Symptoms and cost of functioning; §4 attempts; §8 illness/work interaction; §9 capacity.

**Relation: Confirms and extends.** Current wording: “The broken week-by-week script includes persistent pain, exhaustion, vomiting and incontinence, repeated remedies and disrupted sleep.”

**What should change:** Include treatment access and contraindication fears, stopped or reduced medicines, sedating relief, persistent nausea despite less vomiting, constipation from antiemetics, pain that blocks parenting, and apparent improvement that itself alarms a parent. Keep attributed medicine and risk beliefs distinct from medical fact. Some parents ask for symptom treatment outright because pregnancy-safe measures have failed.

> Everything I read talks about “period-like cramps” and this is nothing like that. On a scale of 1-10 of pain, they are 7-10/10. ## 12. A newborn help plan must allow for illness, readmission, unsafe handovers and a parent who cannot lift, walk, feed or sleep as expected.

**Fits:** §2 first weeks and Recovery, feeding, sleep and bonding; §5 partners; §§10 and 17.4 help plan.

**Relation: Confirms and extends.** Current wording: “Recovery is not just a supplies checklist”

**What should change:** Expand contingency planning beyond the help departure date: a sick toddler can split the household; maternal readmission can leave one adult with a newborn and toddlers; anaemia or injury can remove mobility for weeks; treatment can conflict with the only sleep arrangement that currently works. Include reported dropped babies, sleepwalking, substance-related unsafe handovers and feeding plans that consume bonding time. Do not assume the parent can simply delegate or that impaired early bonding is permanent.

**Verbatim evidence**

<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0084]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0084 -->

I’m in a lot of pain and have been going to my OB every week postpartum. 
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0085]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0085 -->

[pbu33-0094]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0094 -->

<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0095]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0095 -->

<!-- quote-end -->

<!-- quote-end -->

<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0139]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0139 -->

<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0142]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0142 -->

<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0143]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0143 -->
> I'm currently pumping because I still hope this girl will latch. And it's exhausting. I just want to snuggle with my girl but I'm always stressed about getting her to sleep so I can go pump again.  
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0152]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0152 -->
> I feel like I had a somewhat poorly handled induction that resulted in me losing 3x the amount of blood that was normal. My waters were accidentally broken after the first cooks cath balloon was inflated w/ excessive amount of blood, then after pushing i dealt with postpartum hemorrhage. I had a tear that needed stitches but in clinical notes it essentially said there was difficulty with stitching the site and 4 days postpartum my stitches came undone, i had to go to the ER, and am scheduled now for a surgical repair under sedation + antibiotics. I also saw a lactation consultant in the hospital twice that said i was doing great but i found out later my baby was latching too shallow and i discharged with cracked and bleeding nipples that i eventually needed antibiotic/antifungal ointment for and has made my breastfeeding journey hell.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0153]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0153 -->

I feel like he doesnt like me or know me as well as my husband and his family and I feel like my complications in this first month might ruin his bond with me longterm. Does anyone have a similar experience? Do things get better and will my baby be able to bond with me eventually? I’m so heartbroken..
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0160]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0160 -->
> We burnt through all of our emergency fund funding emergency childcare during both hospitalizations, which is also great.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0161]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0161 -->
> For context, I had a very hard pregnancy, gestational hypertension, and an induction that ended in a Category 2 emergency c-section. He also had a tongue tie release two weeks ago. That improved his latch but there’s been no improvement with his reflux. We use Aptamil anti-reflux and normal Aptamil. One night we do normal Aptamil and then the other night the anti-reflux one as the anti-reflux one makes him a bit constipated possibly (he poos once every 24-48 hours instead of multiple times a day now).
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0203]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0203 -->
> My husband delayed going to his office 2 hrs away so I could go to my first appointment, but then got a call back that OB wanted to see me, so my dad left a jobsite to sit with him for me while I went to the appt because there was no way his mom could come get him. Like that is totally unreasonable to me and she didn't even say thank you! I told her that my husband (who works from home) and I were tag teaming her kid because we know she has no backup childcare but if I get any worse, I will not be able to have him here and safely care for him. Her response was well we (meaning husband and I) will have to deal with it because she has no backup. The entitlement is crazy. I can push through with a cold or general sickness, but with this I am seriously down for the count and can't walk more than a few feet without feeling like passing out. <!-- quote-end -->

<!-- quote-end -->

## 13. Money and work can determine whether care, time off, safe recovery or even a pregnancy decision is feasible.

**Fits:** §8; §10 work-and-leave; §§16.5 and 17.2 dated plan.

**Relation: Confirms and extends; provides a concrete planning counter-example.** Current wording: “A calendar is useful to some people but cannot solve the capacity, employment or access problem”

**What should change:** Include employment loss after a restriction note, performance action while pursuing leave, working hours after surgery, insurance gaps, emergency childcare exhausting savings, abortion travel and PTO costs, and therapy that cannot be afforded. Keep the actual one-income trial as contrary evidence to an absence-of-planning account. Dollar comparisons to a low-priced guide do not demonstrate disposable income or product value.

**Verbatim evidence**

[pbu12-0229]

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0229 -->
> My job has a forced PTO policy meaning you must use PTO with ANY missed work, missing work affected my numbers over the past few months but I have been trying my best. My Doctor told me to file for FMLA (I qualify due to the 1 year of work not having to be consecutive). So I asked HR and after dragging their feet they give me the FMLA paperwork. I get a Final written the day AFTER I received my FMLA paperwork. My termination is contingent on my hitting an unattainable number considering we are already over halfway through the month. I literally feel like I have been slapped. I feel worthless. I feel so so stupid. When I asked why I was receiving a final with no other previous write ups I was told “I should have been getting written up this entire time and my manager was incorrect, and that that would be dealt with.”
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu12-0234]

[pbu12-0264]

## 14. Relatives can help, exclude, shame, override boundaries or reopen grief; the mother or ancestor assumed by advice may be absent or unsafe.

**Fits:** §3 stock lines; §5 relatives; §6 visitors and Who to ask; §9 no village.

**Relation: Confirms and extends.** Current wording: “The visitor calendar alone misses bodily privacy, trust and the mother’s ability to recover.”

**What should change:** Include bereavement and protective estrangement, relatives who decline help after promising it, pressured announcements after loss, religion and marriage judgement, grief over a dying relative or pet, and the right to revise an invitation. Preserve helpful mothers, no-pressure support for either pregnancy decision, and regrets about an inflexible visitor ban.

## 15. Identity, eating, exercise and intimacy needs include trauma and mental wellbeing as well as body changes.

**Fits:** §6 Identity and the body; §9 personalization; §§10–11 generic advice.

**Relation: Confirms and extends.** Current wording: “Exercise appears as identity, a hoped-for preparation method, a source of guilt and a question about pain or safety.”

**What should change:** Add eating-disorder recovery, grief about regained freedom and a final pregnancy, wanted sexual desire that has disappeared, fear of emotionally exposing a fetus to sex, and activity or sex restrictions that remove coping tools. Parents can understand physical safety information while needing a different kind of reassurance.

## 16. Substance-use accounts describe dependence, coping, failed cessation, shame, disclosure fears and improvement.

**Fits:** §2 symptoms; §5 disclosure; §6 guilt; new substance-use subsection.

**Relation: Extends.** Current wording: “Fear, shame, self-blame, identity and unwanted expectations”

**What should change:** Name the actual attempts and constraints rather than only exposure anxiety: immediate cessation in one pregnancy can be hard to repeat; quitting can coincide with severe depression; cancer or bereavement can shape coping. Preserve improvement, including a three-day no-vape update, alongside ongoing use. Claims about fetal risk, testing or agency response remain attributed reports.

## 17. Paid or free support is judged by actual help, consent, privacy and follow-through as well as its stated expertise.

**Fits:** §4 doulas, classes, products; §6 trust; §§8 and 11; §17.4.

**Relation: Confirms and extends.** Current wording: “What they are buying is someone to explain and to speak for them”

**What should change:** Add service failure beyond outcome promises: paying double local doula rates, refused attendance, intimate photos and public birth details without consent, an unanswered complaint, and alleged review burial. One free pregnancy-center account weighs useful scans and classes against affiliations and an uncertain agenda. Preserve helpful live-in care and prenatal massage; no account establishes Mamivio-guide demand or efficacy.

## 18. Practical preparation must fit housing, pets, household safety and the parent's actual support network.

**Fits:** §6 basic requests; §9 no village; §10 older-child and first-weeks plan; new home-and-safety material.

**Relation: Extends.** Current wording: “Give second/third-pregnancy buyers an older-child plan for labour day and the first weeks”

**What should change:** Include a one-room or unsafe home, near-term homelessness, hoarded rooms, pet behavior around a fragile newborn, gun storage, a crib inherited without safety knowledge, and ordinary requests for classes, clothing, books or pregnancy buddies. These needs cannot all be put under birth preferences or information overload.

**Verbatim evidence**

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0269 -->

## Outliers and counter-examples

The passages below qualify the main patterns. A good outcome, a reversal of an initial concern or a reported treatment benefit remains one account. Positive and difficult parts can occur in the same account.

**A later pregnancy can be easier, happier or mentally calmer; a bad previous experience does not fix the next outcome.**

[pbu12-0277]

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0277 -->
> I called to tell my husband and we both had a good laugh. I don't care that we're going to be older parents, because I don't feel my age at all and I'm just so excited to get to do this all over again. Once was not enough for me! I'll be 5 weeks on Sunday, so I know that I'm not in the clear yet, which is why we're waiting to tell people, but I'm losing sleep over excitement of getting to shock our family and friends, lol. My boys are currently 14, 13, and 6, so we'll have another 7 year age gap.
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu26-0263]

<!-- quote-start:pbu26-0263 -->
> But here I'm 4 weeks 1 day and just over the moon. I know it's early days but I'm enjoying every minute of it. Our first was conceived in cycle 1, so I thought it was going to be smooth sailing again, little did I know. Not told anybody so neeed to share here!!!
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0370]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0370 -->
> Sorry for my spiel, I just haven't had such good news in such a long time, with my first I was having weekly blood tests, fortnightly Ultrasounds and fortnightly checks at the Maternity Ward, it was nice to head today that so far I will only need to be seen once a month unless things change. 
<!-- quote-end -->

- [pbu12-0277] — A surprise pregnancy brings excitement after infertility, loss and an older-child gap.
- [pbu12-0351] — The author describes improved mood during pregnancy alongside fear about postpartum.
- [pbu26-0263] — Parent expresses early joy after prolonged second-conception effort and loss.
- [pbu26-0271] — Later reply reports earlier delivery, three-day NICU stay and marked maternal relief.
- [pbu33-0256] — Describes growing excitement after learning abortion was unavailable.
- [pbu33-0370] — A low-risk second pregnancy means fewer appointments and relief after intensive monitoring during the first.

**Information and checking can help, and clinicians can validate the decision to seek care.**

[pbu26-0309]

<!-- quote-start:pbu26-0309 -->
> Anyways to make a long story short the ultrasound tech was correct nothing is wrong with my baby I’m officially 5 weeks & 6 days today!
<!-- quote-end -->

[pbu33-0420]

<!-- quote-start:pbu33-0420 -->
> I’ve also been doing a lot of research since the appointment and reading other parents’ experiences has actually made me feel much more hopeful. It seems like mild dilation like this is fairly common and often resolves on its own.
<!-- quote-end -->

- [pbu12-0097] — The author describes prompt emergency treatment and an explanation of changed maternity care.
- [pbu12-0098] — The author describes prompt emergency treatment and an explanation of changed maternity care.
- [pbu12-0201] — The author reports support and stepping away from reassurance loops as helpful.
- [pbu26-0204] — Google ban, yoga and meditation bring reported relief from compulsive searching.
- [pbu26-0232] — Confirmatory outside scan finds heartbeat after author had prepared for D&C.
- [pbu26-0309] — Author reports second opinion reversed suspected loss after irregular-cycle dating uncertainty.
- [pbu26-0314] — Repeated assessment for movement worries is met with compassion, without shaming.
- [pbu33-0019] — Normal monitoring after reduced movement; staff affirm the decision to call.
- [pbu33-0020] — Encourages contacting the provider despite fear of feeling silly.
- [pbu33-0086] — A second pediatrician validates newborn pain and proposes a formula change.
- [pbu33-0420] — Research and other parents' experiences increase hope after a mild kidney finding in a second pregnancy.

**An involved partner and flexible work can make the second newborn transition easier; practical and paid help can improve capacity.**

- [pbu12-0059] — Hiring live-in help follows extreme exhaustion and criticism after birth.
- [pbu26-0053] — Written family boundaries reduce distress before delivery.
- [pbu26-0054] — Partner’s family email assigns him communication around birth.
- [pbu33-0069] — A supportive partner, flexible work, formula feeding and sleep make the second newborn transition easier.

**Positive outcomes and psychiatric improvement appear, but neither establish that every treatment works or erase prior loss.**

- [pbu26-0082] — Medication, therapy and support accompany recovery, with later fluctuations.
- [pbu26-0111] — Parent of six-week-old objects to success stories erasing persistent fear and grief.
- [pbu26-0112] — Other families’ losses remain attached to every reassuring scan.
- [pbu26-0119] — Reports early improvement after Zurzuvae; follow-up only forty-eight hours after finishing.
- [pbu33-0070] — Ten days postpartum, survival-mode distress persists despite good outcomes and practical support.

**Some parents actively request realism or both outcomes; others know the negative outcomes already and want only hopeful, comparable stories.**

[pbu12-0102]

<!-- quote-start:pbu12-0102 -->
> I may regret posting this, as despite the title, I'm looking for realism rather than just being told everything will be fine. For context, I am particularly anxious as I am currently 7 weeks pregnant, and we lost our last baby at 7 weeks.
<!-- quote-end -->

- [pbu12-0102] — At the gestation of an earlier loss, the author asks for realistic reassurance.
- [pbu12-0104] — Early-pregnancy emotional changes feel different from ordinary fatigue and threaten the author's sense of self.
- [pbu26-0044] — Partner explicitly requests honest accounts of both outcomes.
- [pbu26-0161] — Awaiting inaccessible blood test, author requests favorable and unfavorable experiences.
- [pbu33-0191] — Welcomes clinicians avoiding automatic congratulations before confirming a viable pregnancy.
- [pbu33-0226] — Specifically requests positive stories because previous miscarriages already supplied the negative experiences.

**Support can respect a pregnancy decision either way, and a parent can revise a visitor plan or regret over-restricting help.**

- [pbu12-0208] — The author compares friends' different visitor experiences when planning early postpartum help.
- [pbu26-0097] — Supportive partner and practical decision coexist with grief and guilt.
- [pbu33-0346] — A supportive mother welcomes continuation but explicitly leaves the decision to the couple.
- [pbu33-0394] — After a mother's death, asks a helpful mother-in-law to attend birth in grief.
- [pbu33-0395] — Later wants privacy but a partner says withdrawing a birth invitation is unfair to the grandmother.

**The pregnancy-after-loss voice is not automatically grateful, attached or indifferent to gender; these feelings coexist rather than cancel each other.**

- [pbu12-0166] — After losses and severe nausea, a baby's sex brings unexpected disappointment.
- [pbu12-0333] — The author expresses distress about a second boy and fears future exclusion from adult children's lives.
- [pbu26-0167] — After two losses, heartbeat brings numbness and shame rather than expected joy.
- [pbu26-0283] — Parent with loss and infertility history questions distress over fetal sex.

**Aggression is described by pregnant authors and partners who admit causing it, as well as by the people on its receiving end.**

- [pbu12-0138] — A father describes stress, verbal aggression and remorse while supporting another pregnancy.
- [pbu12-0251] — The author describes pregnancy rage, physical aggression and taking responsibility afterward.
- [pbu26-0261] — Father explains deliberate door-punching after pregnant partner withdrew from argument.
- [pbu26-0262] — Partner acknowledges need for therapy while rejecting others’ characterization of his behavior.
- [pbu33-0428] — A partner recognizes becoming intentionally cold or mean to protect himself from his wife's loss-related anxiety.

**A concrete financial plan exists: this couple tests one income before the parent leaves a distressing job; another employer offers useful accommodation.**

- [pbu12-0326] — Work distress prompts a concrete plan to test living on one income before leaving.
- [pbu12-0327] — Work distress prompts a concrete plan to test living on one income before leaving.
- [pbu26-0351] — After leaving work overwhelmed, author receives understanding and scheduling help from boss.

## What fits nowhere

The following material has no dedicated home in the current findings, even when a broad existing heading could technically contain it. Full passages are in the pattern sections above unless reproduced here.

**Birth and postpartum without a living baby need an explicit loss/TFMR/comfort-care branch; the existing first-weeks structure assumes feeding and caring for a newborn.**

- [pbu12-0356] — After one twin dies, the parent faces unfinished preparation and a surviving baby in NICU.
- [pbu12-0357] — After one twin dies, the parent faces unfinished preparation and a surviving baby in NICU.
- [pbu26-0189] — Following stillbirth and C-section, parent grieves never holding her baby.
- [pbu26-0190] — Parent recalls preparing clothes, crib and supplies just before stillbirth.
- [pbu26-0357] — Insurance lapse delays first care to twenty-one weeks, followed by devastating fetal finding.
- [pbu26-0358] — Author requests practical preparation for labor, recovery and grief after anticipated termination.
- [pbu33-0391] — At 32 weeks with trisomy 18, plans comfort care and vaginal birth while seeking induction and stillbirth experiences.
- [pbu33-0396] — Four days after a termination for medical reasons, experiences postpartum changes without a living baby and fears future conception and loss.

**Pregnancy decisions, adoption navigation and reported abortion-access barriers have no dedicated section. Include conflicting preferences, continuation after an initial termination decision, and the author's attempted reversal account without endorsing its claims.**

- [pbu12-0220] — The author weighs a deeply wanted pregnancy against perceived threats to survival.
- [pbu12-0221] — The author weighs a deeply wanted pregnancy against perceived threats to survival.
- [pbu12-0293] — An unwanted pregnancy creates fear about disclosure and receiving support for an abortion.
- [pbu12-0338] — The author reports being refused an abortion and considers having to travel.
- [pbu26-0198] — After injury, eight-month pregnant author needs housing and guidance without giving baby for adoption.
- [pbu26-0254] — Unexpected pregnancy despite Depo prompts search for trustworthy adoption help.
- [pbu33-0281] — Travel, time off, legal restrictions and lack of insurance payment prevent an abortion the couple had considered.
- [pbu33-0296] — At fifteen weeks, asks about ending pregnancy after supportive families had helped an initial decision to continue.
- [pbu33-0329] — After feeling pressured into abortion, contacts an agency offering progesterone and continuing counseling; no outcome is reported.
- [pbu33-0330] — Author reports a progesterone regimen and claimed success odds; these are attributed statements, not verified outcomes.
- [pbu33-0450] — Partner pressure to terminate includes dismissing caution about drug use before a decision is made.
- [pbu33-0451] — Wants medical information before deciding and admits hoping a complication might make the decision easier.

**Trans pregnancy, autism, survivor-controlled disclosure and clinical-exam triggers need explicit personalization and privacy treatment rather than a generic mother/partner selector.**

- [pbu12-0015] — A pregnant transgender man describes discrimination, medication changes, bereavement and fear.
- [pbu12-0307] — A survivor asks about the emotional meaning of sex in pregnancy beyond physical safety.
- [pbu26-0019] — HG admission becomes a traumatic psychiatric-care experience.
- [pbu26-0020] — Autistic author describes how clinicians interpreted communication differences.
- [pbu26-0021] — Hospital experience makes disclosure, birth and future postpartum help feel unsafe.
- [pbu33-0005] — At eight weeks, asks how to disclose a previous assault-related pregnancy privately to the doctor.
- [pbu33-0242] — Past assault makes the clinical nature of pelvic exams frightening; unsure whether disclosure to the doctor is appropriate.

**Teenage parents and their partners are present in the research, regardless of the older findings' target-customer boundary; do not lose their accounts when revising the reader portrait.**

- [pbu26-0369] — Independent young parent had believed medical issues made having children impossible.
- [pbu33-0175] — At 36 weeks, fears exposure to multiple people during labor.
- [pbu33-0347] — At sixteen, fears parental judgement and lacks anyone to ask about an unexpected pregnancy.
- [pbu33-0348] — Two pregnant teenage sisters lack reliable parental support and doubt their ability to support each other.

**Housing before birth, hoarded rooms, pets, firearms and inherited crib safety require a home/safety area; merely choosing registry products does not capture the problem.**

- [pbu26-0198] — After injury, eight-month pregnant author needs housing and guidance without giving baby for adoption.
- [pbu26-0229] — Parents anticipate whether newborn care will leave capacity to notice pets’ illness.
- [pbu26-0361] — Author dates new dog aversion to giving birth, while writing later about a crawling child.
- [pbu33-0091] — An inherited crib was initially judged like ordinary furniture rather than as regulated baby equipment.
- [pbu33-0092] — Family attachment to an inherited crib competes with newly learned concerns.
- [pbu33-0114] — At 19 weeks, inherited hoarding raises concerns about infant safety and health-visitor assessment.
- [pbu33-0115] — An expensive rented storage locker removes little of the household hoard.
- [pbu33-0199] — Sets a gun-storage requirement before the baby's arrival.
- [pbu33-0385] — Despite training and exercise, a boisterous puppy causes fear about sharing space with a newborn.
- [pbu33-0386] — Regrets a puppy's impact on a long-awaited pregnancy and fears losing the ability to focus on the newborn.

**Sexuality includes lost desire and questions about emotional/sexual boundaries with a fetus, not only whether intercourse is physically safe.**

- [pbu12-0297] — The author misses sexual desire and its place in the relationship during pregnancy.
- [pbu12-0307] — A survivor asks about the emotional meaning of sex in pregnancy beyond physical safety.
- [pbu26-0284] — Late bleeding leads to sex restriction, disappointment and unresolved explanation despite reassuring assessment.
- [pbu33-0328] — Research does not remove a fear that sex could cause another miscarriage despite wanting intimacy.

**Substance dependence and cessation, severe psychiatric symptoms and shame about disclosure need named treatment; neither an ordinary-worry heading nor an exposure rule tells these stories.**

- [pbu12-0061] — Two months after traumatic delivery, the author reports intrusive thoughts and unsuccessful treatments.
- [pbu12-0062] — Two months after traumatic delivery, the author reports intrusive thoughts and unsuccessful treatments.
- [pbu12-0344] — At thirty-four weeks, the author describes suicidality, marital conflict and missing the baby shower.
- [pbu12-0359] — The author seeks accountability after repeated attempts to stop vaping during pregnancy.
- [pbu26-0081] — Mother describes insomnia and hallucinations after C-section despite partner taking nights.
- [pbu26-0227] — Second-pregnancy mother cannot repeat immediate smoking cessation and condemns herself.
- [pbu26-0228] — Reply describes unsuccessful quitting and suicidal distress.
- [pbu26-0244] — Financial stress and unaffordable therapy accompany author’s suicidal plan after birth.
- [pbu26-0245] — Pregnant author still wants life and connection despite suicidal distress.
- [pbu26-0340] — Author struggles to stop nicotine vaping after immediately stopping cannabis.
- [pbu26-0341] — Later reply reports three days without vaping.
- [pbu33-0387] — After an unrecognized-pregnancy binge, stops drinking and plans recovery meetings.
- [pbu33-0388] — Online developmental timelines intensify fear about severe drinking before pregnancy recognition.

**Consumer rights in support services include intimate-image consent, public birth-story privacy and complaint follow-through; the service-outcome complaint is incomplete without these.**

- [pbu26-0058] — Paid premium doula rate based on reviews and claimed experience.
- [pbu26-0059] — Doula photographs birth and baby without consent.
- [pbu26-0060] — Unauthorized public birth story compromises mother’s intended privacy.
- [pbu26-0061] — Repeated requests for in-person labor support are refused.
- [pbu26-0062] — Later reply reports submitting complaint and no acknowledgment yet.
- [pbu26-0362] — Later update alleges social-circle reviews bury criticism after consent and support failures.

## Money

This inventory includes every stated price and the share’s purchases, proposed purchases, financial constraints, payment disputes, reported offers and relevant work/coverage costs. It also names service use without a stated transaction, explicitly labelled as such; using a service or product does not establish a paid purchase. Amounts are the authors’ amounts. The expected ER bill is an expectation, the genetic-screening balance is disputed, and a relative’s offer to pay for a baby is reported speech. None is a valuation of this guide.

**All stated amounts and relative prices**

- [pbu12-0024] — Author says the screening test is cheaper; no price stated.
- [pbu12-0079] — Three weeks of unpaid partner leave versus 1.5 planned; tight rent and credit until payday.

## Requests

**Explicit products, resources, plans, practical help and services.** The index includes questions made to the forum, requests already made to a partner or professional, and expressly desired but unavailable care. An index entry is not evidence of commercial demand. The description is the source record’s context; use the cited passage for the exact request and its qualifications.

**Other direct questions and requests for explanation, comparable experiences, reassurance or support.** These are separated from product/service requests: asking whether a symptom is normal, asking for hope or asking for someone’s experience does not mean asking to buy a guide. This supplementary index retains the question-coded records and explicit request language that can otherwise disappear in a commercial reading.
